2026-08-01 ffb1f85f
Verification Status report (reqs vs tested vs verified vs version) + spec 2.33
NEW verification/gen_verification_status.py — the owner-asked evidence report. Scores every requirement into four levels from machine-read sources only (registry + reports/report.json + testlog/test_runs.db): NOT TESTED, TESTED (automated CI test = a claim check against the source), BENCH-TESTED (board-driving tests_mech suite), FIELD-VERIFIED (a CONCLUDED testlog run cites the ID — real hardware), and stamps each hardware proof with the firmware version of the run that produced it. Also emits the locked-but-no-hardware-evidence gap list and a provenance caveat (a suite=sw report cannot yield BENCH results, so that 0 is a statement about the run, not the bench suite). Outputs docs/fluispotter_verification_status.html + reports/ verification_status.json; wired into `make verify` and a standalone `make verification-status`. At fw 0.1.161 / DOC 1.102: 176 requirements, 155 tested, 21 FIELD-VERIFIED, 21 untested. Local site: the report is listed on the Requirements page and its headline appears on the front-page status card (docs/site/ _refresh_data.py now folds the rollup into _status.json; _build_site.py renders it and registers the document). Spec repo 2.33: new §5.6.5 Session Robustness — Bounded Waits, Deadlines & Entry Ordering (SWR-324/325/326/327), covering the silent-wedge closure, the field-found loading-deadline anchor defect (#66) with the "cadence is a test dimension" rule, catch-up policy, the recovery forfeit trail, and the entry kick-settle (#67, tripwire armed). Restored the missing TOC entries for 5.6.3/5.6.4/5.6.5. .docx regenerated; Drive copy updated in place. make verify: 176 requirements, 153 PASS / 0 FAIL. 2026-07-31 9623d854
fw 0.1.161: #67 entry kick-settle (SWR-327) + crumb tripwire stays armed
The entry preload starts the BP (dead-point kick) BEFORE the entry sync (pump-ownership-first, 2026-07-07) — recreating at session entry the SWR-265 kick-vs-flash+WiFi collision fixed at dose boundaries in fw 0.1.104. Entry-WDT fires: run 011 uid 16766117 + closure run uid 16777799 (2 of the last 3 entries), death window bracketed by the 0.1.160 crumbs. Fix: 2.5 s settle (_ENTRY_KICK_SETTLE_MS) between the kick and the entry sync — pump ownership stays first, only the radio+flash burst defers. Mechanism is precedent-backed PLAUSIBLE, not crumb-convicted: 3 crumbed repro entries (incl. one forced parked-pump entry) produced no fire, so per the owner's no-long-runs directive the fix ships now and crumbs 30-36 stay armed as the field tripwire. SWR-327 added (DOC 1.102) + contract test (settle sits between pump start and entry sync). verify: 176 requirements, 153 PASS / 0 FAIL. Regression testlog 2026-07-31-002: table-0 entry, no reboot, sample 0 delta +14 s (best from-parked on record), FINISH. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-07-31 1c2fdf0d
fw 0.1.159: loading deadline anchors at the SCHEDULED moment (#66 field defect)
The SWR-324d gate shipped in 0.1.157 armed at TRIGGER entry — but scheduled samples enter TRIGGER at the 5-min PRELOAD, so the 300 s gate expired right as each schedule moment arrived and samples 1-10 of the 2026-07-31 field run (50x53min battery) were abandoned 8-10 s BEFORE their own scheduled times (hub uids 16777547/16777627/16777701; loading needs ~9 revs >= scheduled_at ~= 2.4 min AFTER schedule). Sample 0 survived (session entry preloads at zero remaining) and every 2-min bench table survived (120 s remaining + 144 s loading just fits 300 s) — the first >5-min-cadence session on 0.1.157+ was the field run itself. Fix: _loading_deadline_ms = max(now, timestamp_ms) + _LOADING_GATE_TIMEOUT_MS; overdue SWR-325 catch-up anchors at now, unchanged. SWR-324 amended (DOC 1.101) with the anchor clause; the contract test now asserts the max(now, scheduled) form and that the entry-anchored form never returns. HUBR-324 limit names fixed to the registry charset. verify: 175 requirements, 152 PASS / 0 FAIL. The abandon machinery performed exactly as designed through all 10 failures: loud synced error each, schedule advanced, flush restored, zero wedges, zero Sync-exceeded warnings in 9.4 h on battery — and hub 1.16.0 auto-generated the broken run's INCOMPLETE report on the concluding reset ack (HUBR-324's first fully-automatic live firing). 2026-07-30 fe14ab68
fw 0.1.157: silent-wedge class closed (SWR-324) — #49/#61 fixed, #50 narrowed
(a) aiorequests.Response now carries the request's per-step timeout onto the BODY read and close via Response._bounded — the last unbounded awaits in the client (a hub sending headers then stalling the body, or a half-open TCP after an AP drop, hung every caller forever with the WDT fed). (b) The 45 s _sync bound is the DEFAULT for every caller (was dose-path-only; the unbounded session-entry sync right after 'Sample 0 scheduled' was the true #49 wedge signature). (c) wlan_scan bounded 45 s — on expiry it reports via serial_sink and returns an empty list so the acquire loop retries loudly instead of parking forever on a wedged driver thread (never raises into the scan loop). (d) The scheduled loading phase is deadlined (5 min, mirroring the commanded deque gate): on expiry the sample is ABANDONED with a loud SYNCED error event and the schedule advances exactly like a completed sample — the session continues, never wedges; doubles as the dose-arm watchdog #61/#49 asked for. SWR-324 added (registry DOC 1.99, Open) with a CI contract test; the requirements doc regenerated. verify: 173 requirements, 150 PASS / 0 FAIL. Regression testlog 2026-07-30-001 on the bench: 3/3 samples to FINISH, zero errors, ZERO 'Sync exceeded' warnings and ZERO loading abandons — the bounds are transparent on the healthy path. Issues: #49 closed (mechanism refuted + real wedge fixed), #61 closed (bound complete client-level + all callers), #50 narrowed to the store-side heartbeat/forensics ask. 2026-07-28 d0242e31
fw 0.1.156: ladder-artifact reset for motion-proven pumps (heal v3 polish)
For a pump the attach motion check PROVED turning, the boot-window residues are false positives: the learned start power (maxed by blind kicks dying on the dead engine) resets to the board_config baseline (4095 - DAC_START_*) and that pump's latched stall/start-FAIL bits (AC 0x5 / BP 0xA) clear before the first drain. Kicked pumps keep ladder + flags untouched — those are real. Verdicts exported as ULPPumpRegulator.last_attach_ac/bp_turning (last_start_mode pattern). Validation (session with one mid-session restart_device injection, scored from hub events): ZERO 'start power raised' events, ZERO flags=15 burst, 3/3 samples to FINISH. Accepted residue: one self-recovered 'BP stall (flags=2)' per attach — the ~16 s batch window straddles the blind period and latches after the one-shot clear; a delayed second sweep could mask real stalls, so it stays (informational: no start-FAIL, no kick, no power raise). 2026-07-28 f20296ce
fw 0.1.152: #60 zero-outage ACCEPTED — attach RTC-I2C heal v2 (testlog 2026-07-28-007/008)
Root cause (run 4, live-convicted on the wedged board): the warm boot's C-runtime peripheral init resets the RTC-I2C engine config, so on the attach path every ULP DAC7574 write died silently from boot onward — kick ladders escalated the register to full power while the motors sat at the DAC's LATCHED pre-reset values (AC below break-away = the run-3 encoder-blind residue; BP masked it by riding its latched flush power). Eliminated on live evidence: pad MCU_SEL theft (re-own proven working), SENS IOMUX_CLK_EN (bit31 on), I2C pad routing (rt1/rt2 healthy), stuck bus (SDA high), dead DAC (bit-bang ACK), AC XTAL-pad inputs 15/16. Fix (v2 after 0.1.151's engine-init-alone FAILED run 5 against the live kicking ULP): full live-cure fidelity in the attach branch — pause both pumps + 150 ms quiesce, _i2c_bus_recover STOP (no register write, latch preserved), _init_rtc_i2c, pad re-own (set_slave_addr + dummy on the fresh engine), resume (fresh kick at learned start power). Acceptance (session 30, fw 0.1.152 + dev.0.24 + IMG-6, /attach_on): TWO warm resets mid-session, both attached; AC revs +6/124 s (expected 6.2 at 20 s/rev, ~3% deficit = the ~2-4 s C-runtime boot pad claim), ring head 11->18 monotone, 9 stamps recovered each time, exactly one MOTOR_START_FAIL_AC per attach (documented kick-ladder restart-from- rest, not a fault), 3/3 samples 3/3 spots to FINISH. IMG-6 -> ACTIVE, IMG-5 -> SUPERSEDED. Also: fw 0.1.150 attach pad forensics (_pad_dump at 4 boot points + rt15/rt16), harness snap() retry + (None,None) contract + PADS baseline + console filter, hub.py bare --command sends '{}' (422 fix), SWR-216/233 ordering tests scoped to the restart segment (the attach branch's engine-only call is by design), prompts.md 253. ULP dev.0.21 / op 1.23.0 — fault-reporting lifecycle rebuilt; two of my own gates were broken
Third adversarial review returned DO-NOT-DEPLOY. Its headline defect was mine and was an over-correction of my own earlier fix. F1 (LIVE, worst). dev.0.18/0.20 added the stall-dedupe LATCH but never RE-ARMED it. Operational regulate_pump cleared recovery_signaled on every good batch, so op reported one event per STALL episode; latching alone turned that into one per COMMAND episode. On a 284 s COLLECT a rotor stalling every ~50 ms would report ONCE — a ~3000x loss of visibility on _diag_stall_count, which is the metric runs are accepted by. This image would have made a degrading pump look clean, which is worse than the wake storm the latch was added to stop. Now re-armed on a CLOSED segment (16 real encoder intervals): a dead motor can never close one, so the storm cannot return. F2 (LIVE). bp_start_signaled was cleared at six episode entries but not at BP_IDLE->BP_RUNNING, so a dose that failed to start carried the flag into the following flush and that run's first start-failure emitted no event and no wake — lost outright, since the CPU read-and-clears motor_stop_events. F3 (LIVE). The AC was the FIFTH stall site: never latched, and still sharing its flag with the kick, which cleared it on every successful start — ~4 wakes/s into the #46 lightsleep path. It now has its own ac_start_signaled and the same latch / re-arm / entry-clear lifecycle as the BP. TWO GATES I WROTE WERE THEMSELVES BROKEN, both found by review, both reproduced: - check_version_bump.py read the FIRST ULP_VERSION block in main.c — the ULP_TEST2 one — so it reported ulp.operational as 1.4.1 while operational was 1.22.0. It could never have caught an unbumped operational stamp, the single thing it exists for. Now anchored per block, with a ulp.testop unit added. - check_map_pairing.py pointed at output/debug/pump_regulator.bin (a stale 1.17.0 snapshot) and three output/pump_*.bin paths that DO NOT EXIST, so it silently skipped them and reported "ok" having verified 1 of 4 pairs — not including the testop pair, the one the pairing rule calls dangerous. Now checks the freshly built artifacts. First run after the fix immediately caught that the .bin files and the address maps were both stale relative to the ELFs, which is exactly its job. All four address maps regenerated from freshly generated symbol tables (the .sym files were stale for main/test/test2; only the testop path regenerated its own). All four pairs now verify: operational 1.23.0, testop 9.0.21, test 1.2.14, test2 1.4.1. STILL NOT DEPLOYED, and dev.0.21 has not itself been reviewed. Every review in this series has found at least one live defect, including two that would have required a power cycle and one that would have hidden a failing pump. ULP dev.0.20 / op 1.22.0 — fix a build break, a truncation and a half-done fix
A fact-check of my own status report found three defects. Two were mine and had shipped undetected across two commits. (a) BUILD BREAK (from dev.0.18). bp_start_signaled was declared inside #ifdef ULP_FOLD_BUILD but cleared from entry helpers that also compile for the TEST/TEST2 diagnostic images, where that macro is deliberately not defined. Both images stopped compiling. build_verify_ulp.py was exiting 1 the whole time and I did not see it, because I piped it through grep — and grep masks the exit code. That trap is already written down in my own notes. The declaration is now outside the guard; all four images build. (b) TRUNCATION, from two changes interacting. dev.0.19 narrowed us_to_ticks to uint32 after proving the FUNCTION bit-exact over the uint32 domain — but did not audit the callers, and the dose backstop passes a uint64 budget. dev.0.17's DOSE_DEADLINE_MULT 10->30 is what pushes that value past 2^32 us: at SPR 600 s the 10,649 s deadline silently collapsed to 2,059 s. Neither change alone does this. This is precisely the failure the one-change-per-image rule exists to prevent, and I stacked nine changes across three commits. Now clamped rather than truncated; losing precision on an absurd configuration is safe because the firmware's blood_pump_timeout owns the real verdict. (c) HALF-DONE FIX. dev.0.18 claimed the "dose and park stall branches latch the dedupe". Only RUNNING and DOSE did. PARK and PARK_OFFSET kept the guard with no assignment, so they still emitted MOTOR_STOP_BP + wakeup_main_cpu() on every stalled pass — the wake storm the fix was written to stop. All four fold sites now latch; the two non-fold sites are left alone as they match historical operational behaviour. Build (all four images, build_verify run UNPIPED and exit code checked): operational 1.22.0 7108 B stack need 340 gate 442 headroom 532 (ok) test 1.2.14 5640 B headroom 2004 (ok) test2 1.4.1 4364 B headroom 3540 (ok) build_verify still exits 1 on the byte-identical check alone: op differs from the committed output/debug/pump_regulator.bin, which is 1.17.0 and not the deployed board binary either. That check is comparing against a stale reference and should be re-baselined or scoped when op is next promoted. STILL NOT DEPLOYED. Nothing in dev.0.17..0.20 has run on hardware. 2026-07-27 8f3f3488
ULP dev.0.19 / op 1.21.0 — us_to_ticks to 32-bit arithmetic (+48 B), verified bit-exact
A type-width audit of the ULP found exactly ONE libgcc helper in the linked image: __ashldi3 (40 B), pulled in by udiv64's `den << shift` where shift is a RUNTIME value — not an RV32 instruction. udiv64 inlines into four hot functions (us_to_ticks, seg_pass, seg_stalled, bp_enter_dosing), each paying two helper calls per iteration across 45 iterations. us_to_ticks is now a shift-subtract over the 64-bit numerator held as two 32-bit words, using 32-bit operations only. `us` is uint32 (4295 s of range; the largest constant in the file is PARK_SAFETY_TIMEOUT_US = 30 s), the quotient fits uint32 (2^32 us / 7.35 us-per-tick = 5.8e8), and `period` is ~3.86e6 < 2^22 so `rem << 1` cannot overflow. VERIFIED before writing the code, not after: the exact algorithm was modelled and compared against the 64-bit reference over the full uint32 `us` domain — edge cases plus 4000 random values, x 6 period values — 0 mismatches in 24084 cases. CORRECTION TO THE AUDIT that motivated this: it recommended also replacing `x1000 / PULSES_PER_REV` with the exact identity `x125 / 648` to retire udiv64 and __ashldi3 entirely, rating it SAFE with a "peak numerator 1.2e9". Independent checking shows that peak assumes pulse_count = 16. The DOSE BUDGET site multiplies by bp_dose_target instead (3067 for COLLECT), giving 2.3e11 at the config-max 600 s/rev — a uint32 overflow. The identity itself IS exact (1000/5184 == 125/648, verified over a full grid); the range claim is not. Retiring udiv64 needs a bounded or clamped dose budget first, and belongs in its own image. Also not taken here: converting the whole tick domain to uint32 (a further -386 B .text / -76 B .bss). uint32 ticks wrap at 8.77 h, and 50 sites compare absolute values (`rtc_ticks() >= deadline`) which is NOT wrap-safe; all 50 must move to the difference form `(int32_t)(now - dl) >= 0` atomically. A partial conversion is silently wrong and smaller than the correct one, which is exactly the trap to avoid. TESTOP dev.0.19: 7092 B, stack need 340 B, gate 442 B, headroom 548 B (ok — first time above the warn threshold, not merely above the fail line). 2026-07-27 f7a04d94
ULP dev.0.18 / op 1.20.0 — fix the three DO-NOT-DEPLOY defects from the adversarial review
The adversarial review of dev.0.17 returned DO NOT DEPLOY. All three defects are fixed here, plus two budget recoveries. BLOCKER, now REVERTED — dev.0.17 cancelled BP_PARK_CAL on `test_request == TEST_NONE`. TEST_NONE (0) is the RESTING value of test_request, not a cancel signal. Consequences, both confirmed from the tooling: on TestOp, board_script sets bp_park_calibrating and never touches test_request, so the cancel fired on the first pass and the motor never turned — calibration broken outright. On operational the same branch never cleared bp_park_calibrating, which the BP_IDLE dispatcher re-enters on, so BP_IDLE -> BP_PARK_CAL -> cancel -> BP_IDLE looped at the 1 ms wake period with a bit-banged DAC write and a wakeup_main_cpu() every iteration — past the 30 s backstop (the cancel is tested first and the deadline is re-armed each entry) and clearable only by a power cycle. That is the #46 lightsleep kill path at maximum rate, permanently, and it was reachable by exactly the host-timeout scenario the change was written for. The stale-test_request concern is real but needs a token that is not the idle value. bp_start_signaled is now cleared at EVERY episode entry (6 sites). dev.0.17 introduced it but cleared it only on a successful kick, so the FIRST BP start-failure was reported and every subsequent one was silent — and since the CPU read-and-clears motor_stop_events, a suppressed re-set is lost, not delayed. On a jammed pump the operator would have seen bare timeouts with no start-FAIL evidence, on the device where that bit is the primary jam diagnostic. The dose and park stall branches now LATCH bp_recovery_signaled. Moving the kick onto its own flag removed the incidental gating those branches relied on, so every stalled pass emitted MOTOR_STOP_BP + wakeup_main_cpu() at ~3.6/s — the exact wake storm the commit claimed to fix, made worse by the 3x longer dose backstop. The past-deadline dose sleep now yields to a running AC. Placed ahead of the AC_RUNNING check it dropped an actively regulating AC from continuous polling to one seg_pass per 1 ms wake, Nyquist-marginal against its ~1.93 ms transition period; undercounted intervals read as "too slow" and drive AC power UP — the same undercount family as the calibration bug. Kick ordering is now encoder ON -> DAC write -> motor ON. Keeping DAC-before-enable (so a reload cannot drive at the pre-reset power) while powering the encoder first means the ~1 ms DAC write doubles as encoder settling, instead of latching kick_level inside the turn-on transient against an ENCODER_SKIP_PULSES budget of exactly 1. BUDGET (+92 B): the #56 park stop-verify is DELETED, not repaired — it disabled the encoder pad and then busy-spun counting edges on it, and init_input leaves both pulls off, so it read a float: bp_parked was granted by a check that never ran and its MOTOR_STOP_BP could never fire. Measuring before de-energising does not work either (DOSE_COAST_US is 1 s and coast-down edges are expected). bp_parked reverts to the operational criterion that shipped through the 40-hour run. Also removes a 50 ms BLOCKING busy-spin from the "nothing blocks" fold. seg_stalled now caches its window instead of recomputing calc_batch_timeout_us + us_to_ticks (64-bit maths) every pass. Also removed two comments referencing HALT_REASSERT_US, a macro that does not exist because that feature was removed, and corrected the seg_pass comment that promised a 1 Hz DAC re-assert the code does not implement. TESTOP dev.0.18: 7144 B, stack need 340 B, gate 442 B, headroom 500 B. NOT DEPLOYED — re-review pending, since these fixes came after the review that produced them. 2026-07-27 6772cfec
ULP dev.0.17 / op 1.19.0 — six audit fixes; the seventh does not fit in RTC memory
Six CONFIRMED findings from the 3-pass behavioural audit and runs 2026-07-26-002/003: 1. DOSE_DEADLINE_MULT 10 -> 30. The ULP's dose backstop was ~22.2 s on the 50 deg PREPARE while the firmware waits 66.7 s, so for 44 s the pump was commanded to dose, the ULP had stopped kicking (main.c gates the whole kick+drive block on the deadline) and emitted no further MOTOR_START_FAIL_BP. That is the mechanism behind the 67-second dead window in the aborted run. 30x matches the firmware exactly. 2. The kick writes the DAC BEFORE energising the motor. Raising enable first left a ~1 ms window driving at whatever the DAC7574 still latched — after a CPU reload that is the PRE-RESET power. 3. A dose past its deadline now SLEEPS instead of spinning the core at 100 % duty for the remainder of the firmware timeout. 4. BP RUNNING stall LATCHES bp_recovery_signaled (the assignment operational had and the fold dropped) and the kick gets its OWN persistent flag. Previously a marginal pump emitted MOTOR_STOP_BP + wakeup_main_cpu() ~3.6x/s where op emitted one per episode — ungated wakes are the #46 lightsleep path. The flag is file-scope, NOT a per-call local: try_kick_pass runs every loop pass, so a local would signal every 50 ms and make it ~6x worse. 5. Park-cancel uses bp_do_stop() instead of a hand-rolled halt that left has_started, recovery_signaled, integral, prev_error and the max/min power sentinels stale. 6. BP_PARK_CAL honours TEST_NONE as a cancel, so a firmware that hits its 60 s timeout can actually stop the motor, and a stale test_request cannot re-trigger the calibration from BP_IDLE later. NOT FIXED — the 1 Hz hardware HALT re-assert. Implemented, measured, REMOVED: it does not fit. With it the image had 348 B headroom against a 340 B worst-case stack (8 B of real margin) versus a 442 B computed gate. Three variants were tried (per-pump helper, single loop-top deadline, DAC-write-only) and none got under budget. The finding it addresses is SUSPECTED, not CONFIRMED, so it lost the budget contest. This is now an RTC-MEMORY problem: the fold plus these fixes exceeds the 8 KB budget. TESTOP dev.0.17: size 7184 B, stack need 340 B, gate 442 B, headroom 476 B (thin). NOT DEPLOYED — adversarial code review first, per the owner's instruction. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>
2026-07-27 3703cdd6
Gate: a ULP binary must be PROVABLY paired with its address map
WHY: I cited a "1.15.0 rollback pair" repeatedly today as a zero-cost recovery. It was not a pair. The binary was genuine 1.15.0; the map saved beside it carried ULP_VARIANT='testop' — a testop-era map. Deploying the two together gave a board reading every shared variable at the wrong address: ULP_VERSION decoded 0.19.168, the overflow flag read 6056, the ULP looked DEAD. Nothing detected it. I found it by hand, after twice mis-reading a cached module as a deployment failure. That failure mode is worse than misreporting: the firmware WRITES pump commands through these addresses, so a mismatched pair can poke arbitrary RTC memory, and the documented consequence is a clobbered stack canary. THE CHECK is the same self-validating read that finally exposed it on hardware, done offline: the map states where ULP_VERSION lives, so read that offset out of the .bin and decode it. A correct pair yields a sane MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH; a mismatched one yields garbage. Family is checked against SWR-503 as amended (MAJOR 1 = operational, 9 = TestOp). NEGATIVE CONTROL (this is the part that makes it trustworthy): bad pair (1.15.0 bin + testop-stamped map) -> 0.19.168 <- the exact garbage the board showed good pair (1.15.0 bin + map from git a96f166) -> 1.15.0 So the gate provably catches the specific mistake that motivated it, rather than being asserted to work. Wired into `make verify` and tools/hooks/pre-commit alongside the version-bump and test-symbol gates. NOTE, and it needs an owner decision later: the BOARD now runs operational 1.15.0 (restored, self-validated by ULP_VERSION reading 1.15.0 on a fresh boot) while the REPO's operational pair still decodes 1.17.0 — the fold. Deploying from the repo would put the fold straight back on the device. That divergence is deliberate for now (fold parked on the TestOp track per the 2026-07-20 freeze rule) but it is a loaded gun and should not be left indefinitely. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>
2026-07-26 f3f45194
Register run 2026-07-26-003 (resumed 20x2, 20/20 with 25 faults) + correct the ratchet claim
After the recovery power-cycle the firmware restored SESSION from state.dat and finished the 20x2 schedule unattended: 20/20 samples, 41 doses, FINISH, 25 faults, 1 boot. Fault mix: BP stall x18, BP start-FAIL x2, three AC-involved, 2 unclassified. Only full-length 2-minute-spacing dataset on dev.0.15. CORRECTION I OWE: bp_kick_reg is NOT the monotonic ratchet I described repeatedly today. Observed 1095 (x7) -> 0 -> 495 -> 0 -> 0 -> 0. Escalation drives the register to 0 (FULL power) and persists it (main.c:2670-2677), but bp_seed_pid then adopts the live regulated DAC back into the same register (main.c:2102-2110), raising it again. Net behaviour is escalate-to-full-then-adopt-back, oscillating. My "1095 -> 495 -> 295 -> 0 ratchet" captured only the escalating half. This matters for the fix: anything that assumes a one-way climb (a simple decay) fights the adopt-back path rather than the escalation — which is precisely how dev.0.14 destabilised the AC. Both halves exist in the operational build, so still not fold-specific. Second finding: the schedule COMPLETES despite the fault load — every fault self-recovered and no sample was lost. So 2-minute spacing is a RELIABILITY failure, not an availability one, and sample-count acceptance would have passed this run while the pump was repeatedly failing to start and driving at maximum. Acceptance must score fault RATE and bp_kick_reg excursions, not samples alone. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>
2026-07-26 663baca3
testlog: evidence rule accepts run data, not only file:line — and the last 2 findings
The findings bar I added this session REJECTED my own process finding because its evidence was run data rather than a source reference. GATES (tools/gates, run by `make verify`, by CI, and by tools/hooks/pre-commit via core.hooksPath — every rule broken on 2026-07-26 was one already written down and recitable, so this enforces at the only moment that reliably matters): 1. check_version_bump.py — hash the source EXCLUDING its version block; if the hash moved and the version did not, fail. The ULP stamp was left unbumped THREE times in one session after the hazard was documented twice. 2. check_test_symbols.py — resolve the #ifdef guards for the SHIPPING build and fail on any test asserting a function the guards compile OUT. The suite reported "143 PASS / complete parity" while asserting on sample_encoder, which does not ship. NOTE: a first version used the ELF symbol table and produced false positives (nm cannot tell "#ifdef-ed out" from "static and inlined" — it flagged ac_tick/run_simple_p, which DO ship). A gate with false positives gets bypassed, so it now resolves guards in source. 3. testlog.add_finding() REFUSES a finding without verdict (CONFIRMED/SUSPECTED/ REFUTED), evidence containing a file:line, and a concrete failure_scenario. Four mechanisms were asserted on 2026-07-26 from a stale comment, one deduped log line and a timing coincidence; each would have failed this gate. F3, which I flagged CRITICAL, is REFUTED: a 50 ms kick window holds 16-50 edges and needs 2, at most one AC write can land in it, and seg_pass compares LEVELS so a 1 ms block cannot hide a 3.09 ms edge. I also called the transfer "bit-banged" (it is the RTC hardware I2C master) and offered corroboration that only proved the AC was regulating. F4's defect claim is refuted and its op comparison was false. F1's conclusion holds but its evidence was wrong line numbers plus a comment quoted as code. The "calibration contract proven end-to-end" claim holds only because the firmware idles the BP first. - RUNNING stall dedupe never latches (`*recovery_signaled = true` dropped) and the shared flag is passed into the kick which clears it -> ~3.6 ungated CPU wakes/s into the #46 lightsleep path, where op emitted one per episode. - Stale recovery_signaled can suppress MOTOR_START_FAIL/MOTOR_STOP for a whole episode, so "only one escalation logged" cannot distinguish stopped-vs-unreported. - The turn-on skip is per-ARM not per-WINDOW, so from window 2 a single edge fakes a start. - Post-deadline BP_DOSING spins at 100% duty AND blocks calibration. - AC service during a BP dose lost its rate limit, 150 ms cap and the 160-edge AC_BUSY_DOSE_GUARD that existed to keep I2C off the target crossing. 2026-07-26 01755752
ULP fold behavioural audit (PARTIAL): 1 regression, 1 defect, 2 of my claims refuted
Audit method: compare the operational function's ACTUAL CODE, statement by statement, against the fold. Comments in main.c are treated as unverified until the code confirms them — this audit exists because a stale comment sent me down a wrong root cause twice in one session. F1 — try_kick_start step 4b is NOT dropped. My claim was WRONG. The header at main.c:762 says "stop the motor (DAC->HALT, motor-enable LOW)"; the CODE at main.c:1651 says "Step 4b (1.9.0): do NOT de-energize", with bench evidence that a pulsed kick produced ZERO edges from the sealed dead-point while a continuous drive at power 3000 turned a clean revolution. The fold matches the real op behaviour. The header is stale documentation — fix the comment, not the code. Reverting to a pulsed kick would reintroduce the failure 1.9.0 fixed. F2 — the fold's start bar is MORE permissive than op, not stricter. Op calls sample_encoder(pulse_target=1) and accepts on count>0; sample_encoder discards 1 turn-on edge, latches t_start on the next, then counts 1 more => op requires THREE rising edges in 50 ms. Fold dev.0.14/0.15 requires two; dev.0.11-0.13 required one. This REFUTES my "two-edge bar is too strict" hypothesis, and shows the single-edge accept was the real outlier. F3 — CRITICAL fold regression: the kick's edge watch is no longer isolated. Op detects the start inside sample_encoder, a TIGHT BLOCKING busy-poll where nothing else on the ULP runs, so every transition is seen. The fold samples once per main-loop pass while the same loop services ac_tick, whose seg_pass does a bit-banged ~1 ms I2C DAC write on every AC segment close. The BP encoder is unobserved for that ~1 ms, and at kick speeds an encoder period is shorter than that, so real edges can be missed - start never confirmed, escalate, repeat, while the motor may actually be turning. The failing run corroborates: the AC was regulating during the BP start-FAIL (ac_dac=2429 pwr=1666 spr=20.0). This is a lost IMPLICIT guarantee (blocking = exclusive encoder access) - exactly the class a function-list parity check cannot see. F4 — seg_pass refreshes stall_edge BEFORE the jitter skip, so an edge the code explicitly classifies as "not a real motion edge" still resets the stall timer. Op has no equivalent: it decides stall from count==0 returned by sample_encoder, not a rolling timestamp. PENDING, explicitly NOT clean: regulate_pump, bp_drive_window, ac_busy_regulate/ ac_busy_hall_service, stop_pump's full side-effect set, test_determine_parking vs BP_PARK_CAL, the enter_* actions, and sched_idle's sleep gating. Process finding: the original parity check compared function LISTS and reported complete parity. It cannot detect a lost timing guarantee (F3) or a reordered statement (F4), and F1 shows reading even the documented contract is unsafe. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>
2026-07-26 ee52c7b4
Run 2026-07-26-001 PASS — encoder turn-on skip confirmed as the root cause
10/10 samples to FINISH on TestOp dev.0.15, 20 doses, 0 reboots, 2 faults, ZERO BP faults. The decisive number never moved: bp_kick_reg held the baked nominal 895 for the whole run. On 1.17.0 it ratcheted 1095 -> 495 -> 295 -> 0 (full power) and the BP visibly over-sped. The only change here was 895 -> 1095, which is power going DOWN via the existing bp_seed_pid() adoption path — not escalation. faults 43 (2.15/sample) -> 2 (0.20/sample) BP faults 43 -> 0 kick ratchet 1095 -> 0 -> none calibration failed attempt 1 -> passed first attempt Mechanism, evidenced end to end: the encoder is powered down between drives and during every ladder hold, so its first edge after power-on can be turn-on jitter rather than rotation. The old sample_encoder discarded ENCODER_SKIP_PULSES such edges; the fold dropped the guard. A jitter edge then declared the motor started while the rotor stood still -> no further edges -> seg_stalled -> re-kick -> ESCALATE, permanently. Restoring the skip means the chain never starts. This also resolves Sten's "stalls happen exactly at park position, seems systematic": the drive re-arm after an encoder power-cycle is where a jitter edge lands, and park is where those power-cycles bunch up. The repeatable rotor angle was pointing at the ENCODER, not at mechanics. Both remaining faults are AC-only (BP idle and halted in each). The start-FAIL may be a side effect of the fix itself — the skip requires TWO rising edges before declaring a start, and the first edges off rest are far slower than the 3.9 ms/edge of steady state. If AC start-FAILs recur, apply ENCODER_SKIP_PULSES to the drive re-arm only and leave the kick at one edge. Also verified: SWR-286 session counter increments once per session start and persists — 14, 15, 16, 17, 18 across the last five sessions. Not proven here: TestOp, USB attached, bench. Operational promotion still needs the op binary re-baselined and a battery/PPK2 run with USB detached. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>
2026-07-26 4f59a91c
build_verify_ulp: replace the fixed 768 B stack bar with a per-build computed gate
Sten's observation: the headroom constant was sized for a DEEP call stack, and the fold doesn't have one any more. Measured from the linked op image (frame sizes from `add sp,sp,-N` prologues, call graph from `jal`, longest path): 20 B __start 128 B main <- the continuous loop 48 B bp_drive_pass 96 B seg_pass 32 B dac7574_write_control 48 B ulp_riscv_i2c_master_write_to_device ------ 372 B worst case, 6 frames So the real requirement is 372 B while the gate demanded 768 B — more than 2x over-provisioned, and it was blocking images that carry ample margin. The constant described the PRE-FOLD chain (bp_tick -> regulate_pump -> sample_encoder -> dac7574 -> i2c, with ac_busy_regulate alongside); dropping regulate_pump removed the deepest nesting along with 800 B of code. New gate derives itself per build: FAIL headroom < max(need * 1.3, need + 64) WARN headroom < max(need * 1.5, need + 128) (reported as "thin") 1.3x covers observed compiler drift (frames move 16-32 B across rebuilds) plus one unforeseen ~48 B frame; the 4 B canary remains the hard backstop. Because `need` is recomputed from the image, the gate TIGHTENS ITSELF automatically if anyone re-nests the call graph — which a fixed constant can never do. Result: operational need=372 gate=483 headroom=508 (thin, PASS) test need=260 gate=338 headroom=1884 (ok) test2 need=260 gate=338 headroom=3424 (ok) Sound because the ULP has no recursion (guarded), no indirect calls, no libgcc helpers (checked separately) and RISC-V has no red zone. It does NOT model an async frame pushed by the ROM wake path — a stack low-water probe should confirm it empirically before this is leaned on for an operational release. Precedent: ULP 1.14.0's trust gate measured 228 B low-water. I had also been quoting the bar as 512 B in discussion; the code said 768. Corrected here. 2026-07-26 dfc2ef68
ULP dev.0.15 / op 1.18.0 — withdraw the two power-steering fixes; keep the two conservative ones
dev.0.14 shipped four changes at once. Two of them STEER POWER — re-seed Simple-P from nominal on every successful kick (handover), and decay the learned kick power back toward nominal — and they destabilised the AC immediately: on the bench the pump moved in tiny jerks 2-3 per second during the anticoagulant prime. That cadence convicts them. At the AC's 20 s/rev the measurement burst is ~70 ms (16 intervals at 3.9 ms/edge) and the ladder holds 256/512 ms at levels 7-8, so 330-500 ms IS the regulation cycle. The motor was stepping once per CONTROL UPDATE, not once per revolution — the operating point was being reset every cycle. Root of the mistake: escalation is load-bearing on this hardware. Nominal power is marginal (the same fact the park hard spot demonstrates), so the escalated power is what keeps the pump turning. Punishing every SUCCESSFUL start with a decay turns it into an oscillator: start nominal -> too weak -> stall -> kick escalates -> decay pulls it back down -> stall. 1.17.0 ratcheted one way and over-sped; dev.0.14 oscillated. Both are the same underlying fact seen from opposite sides. KEPT (conservative — they only suppress FALSE signals, they cannot steer power): (1) encoder turn-on skip in both the kick and the drive re-arm; (4) BP_PARK_OFFSET stall detection. The turn-on skip is exonerated by the same timing that convicts the others: it discards 1 edge in ~17 at 3.9 ms/edge, far too small to produce a visible step. PROCESS: stacking four changes in one image broke my own standing rule (one change per image, boot-prove each) — the rule I have written down for MCU config and did not apply to ULP control changes. The pump reported it within one prime. Op headroom 420 -> 508 B (the dead handover GC'd out); still 4 B under the 512 B bar, so op promotion stays blocked. TestOp (~1.8 KB spare) is unaffected. ULP 1.18.0 — four fixes for the run 2026-07-25-001 stall/over-speed chain (NOT deployable yet: op headroom 420 < 512)
Owner direction: keep Simple-P with frequent regulation on park + dose (no ladder there), and do NOT add a power floor — the ladder and Simple-P are what hold speed. (1) ENCODER TURN-ON SKIP RESTORED — the driver of everything else. The encoder is powered down during a ladder hold and between drives; its first edge after power-on can be turn-on jitter, not rotation. The old sample_encoder discarded ENCODER_SKIP_PULSES such edges, and Test2 1.4.1 raised its start bar for exactly this ("a single encoder turn-on-jitter edge FAKED STARTS — 'Started 2 ms, run edges 0'"). The fold dropped the guard, so a jitter edge declared the motor started while the rotor stood still -> no further edges -> seg_stalled -> re-kick -> ESCALATE. Now discarded in BOTH the kick (try_kick_pass) and the drive re-arm (seg_reset/seg_pass), so a jitter edge can neither fake a start nor corrupt the interval timing. (2) SANE HANDOVER — a successful kick re-arms first_update so Simple-P re-seeds from the nominal start power instead of inheriting the escalated kick power the motor was started at. (3) DECAY — a successful start relaxes the learned kick one escalate-step back toward the baked nominal. Escalation previously only ever rose, and bp_seed_pid() derives start_power FROM it, so one hard spot permanently over-powered every later drive: measured 1095 -> 495 -> 295 -> 0 (FULL power) across a single 20-sample session. (4) BP_PARK_OFFSET STALL DETECTION — the centre-coast into the sealed park position had none, so a rotor stalled exactly there (where the owner observes the stalls) was never reported and the motor was held energised against it for the full 5 s backstop. Detected via the existing deadline. WHY THIS SLIPPED PAST VERIFICATION: test_ulp_source.py asserts on sample_encoder, which still exists in main.c under #ifndef ULP_FOLD_BUILD and is compiled OUT of the shipping binary. The tests kept passing against the rolled-back path while the code that ships was never checked — exactly the category-B gap already written up in docs/ulp_1.17.0_verification_status.md, where SWR-126 (the kick / sample_encoder requirement) is listed. My op-parity review also compared function LISTS, not the guards inside each function. NOT DEPLOYED. Operational headroom 596 -> 420 B, below the 512 B FAIL bar (the #48/#36 stack-overflow class). Reclaimed 340 -> 380 -> 420 by merging the two turn-on counters into one seg_t field, detecting the park-offset stall from the existing deadline instead of new stall machinery, and decaying toward compile-time nominals instead of runtime-captured ones. Still short. Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>

Switched by ONE macro near the top of main.c: #if !defined(ULP_TEST_BUILD) && !defined(ULP_TEST2_BUILD) #define ULP_FOLD_BUILD 1 #endif 24 fold guards renamed ULP_TESTOP_BUILD -> ULP_FOLD_BUILD, so op and TestOp now share the fold. As the operational binary it now answers that contract directly — BP_IDLE enters BP_PARK_CAL on `bp_park_calibrating || test_request == TEST_DETERMINE_PARKING`, and EVERY exit (cancel / 30 s deadline / success / sweep-fail) clears test_request = TEST_NONE. That clear is required twice over: it is the firmware's completion signal, and leaving it set would re-trigger cal from BP_IDLE on the next pass — an endless loop. 2026-07-25 54511239
ULP dev.0.12 — hall monitored constantly: SWR-289 strict deque-on-tick in every state
bp_hall_edge_service is hoisted to the TOP of the continuous loop — one call per pass, in EVERY state — so the crossing ring/deque is updated on every hall tick. The tick-model per-state calls counted hall only in RUNNING + PARKING, so the folded DOSING/COAST/OFFSET/CAL were hall-blind (op's bp_drive_window had counted them). Counting is unconditional; the CPU wake stays gated to BP_RUNNING. 2026-07-25 70e2d1ea
ULP dev.0.11 — CLEAN fold: fully non-blocking (per-pass kick) + skip ladder back
Closes out the fold (Sten: "Skip ladder and fold in the kick to be non-blocking — we want this clean"). (1) The LAST blocking helper is gone. try_kick_start's <=50 ms blocking sample_encoder wait is folded into a per-pass first-edge kick (try_kick_pass) — the SAME escalating continuous hold (drive at *dac_start, +power+persist every ~50 ms without a first edge, motor never de-energized, SWR-132/1.9.0) spread across continuous-loop passes. With no caller left, --gc-sections drops try_kick_start AND sample_encoder: the ULP now blocks NOWHERE. bp_dose_park_kick is #ifdef-split so op keeps try_kick_start (byte-identical). (2) The skip ladder is restored (battery profile). The pure-continuous dev.0.10 loop spun the CPU whenever a pump ran. dev.0.11 re-adds the adaptive skip in folded AC/BP RUNNING: after each closed Simple-P segment the encoder is held OFF for the ladder interval as a WALL-CLOCK deadline (robust to loop speed — a count would blow through while the other pump spins the loop), and sched_idle() sleeps the loop between measurement bursts (hall still serviced per wake). Dose/park/cal never skip, so they stay edge-exact + gap-free. Headroom 1804 -> 956 B (still 2x the cal peak). On-board (canary clean): park -> parked=1, cal enc_total 5162 ~= 5184 in-gate (bracket 360, offset 180), BP+AC steady regulation through the ladder. 2026-07-25 da95e0ed
ULP dev.0.10 FULL FOLD: all states per-pass, drop the blocking regulators
Rebuild every ULP state on one non-blocking per-pass primitive (seg_pass + the unchanged run_simple_p law), dropping bp_drive_window + regulate_pump + sample_encoder + ac_busy_* (~1.5 KB) via --gc-sections -> testop stack headroom 368 -> 1804 B (4x the cal's spin-up peak). 2026-07-24 1796ec7b
scheduler design: resolve stale 'open question' para — endpoint decided HYBRID
2026-07-24 e75e99a3
scheduler design §2.1a: PROBE RESULT — ULP cannot read PCNT (hybrid decided)
Built a TESTOP peripheral-read probe (TEST_PEEK loads *(uint32_t*)peek_addr from the ULP; probe-only mode, no canary confound) and ran it live. The ULP reads RTC-domain registers exactly (both control magics) but every HP/APB read fails — PCNT reads returned the ADDRESS ITSELF (0x600170FC for addr 0x600170FC), the signature of a load that never reaches the bus. Proven: the S3 ULP RISC-V cannot access HP peripherals; PCNT is unreachable from the ULP. => the fully non-blocking counter-compare endpoint is NOT feasible; the redesign is bounded to the HYBRID (bounded bit-bang poll windows + fair dispatch). All correctness wins (#48/#55/#56) survive. Reusable probe saved to tools/ulp_probe/. ULP source reverted clean (probe was TESTOP-only, one-shot).
2026-07-24 1bb57096
design note §2.1: hardware-counter spike — it's CPU-side, not ULP-side
Git archaeology of Mirza's counter work: PCNT was implemented May-Jun 2025 as CPU-side esp32.PCNT (custom mpy build) during CPU-regulation era; NO ULP commit has ever used a hardware counter; the ULP RISC-V regulator (1aa9d2c, 2026-04-02) has always bit-banged (5184 steps/rev, gpio_get_level). 'Hardware counters available to the ULP' is a conflation. Corrected the note's false premise; the live open question is now whether the S3 ULP can read PCNT at all (TRM + bench probe), which decides pure-loop vs hybrid. Correctness fixes need no counter.
2026-07-24 74ee7e35
design note: ULP cooperative non-blocking scheduler (branch start)
Captures the architecture Sten proposed — single flat non-blocking main loop, hardware edge counters (Mirza-validated), MoveTo/SimpleP/hall-flag dispatch — as the structural cure for #48/#55/#56 (the multi-second dose/coast/park blocking windows are the shared root). Includes the adversarial check on 'never sleep': the correctness win is NON-BLOCKING (orthogonal to sleeping); continuous spin helps only stop-precision latency during active motion and wastes battery during idle — so 'don't sleep while working, sleep while idle', state-driven. Plus loop-rate analysis (counting decoupled from rate by HW counters; rate set by stop precision), #53 caution (no ULP-side clock defense), RTC-bus-contention + DAC-write-on-change, and a staged TestOp migration. ULP TestOp dev.0.2: #48 clock-gate conviction probe
Workflow-verified mechanism: SENS.sar_peri_clk_gate_conf.iomux_clk_en (0x60008904 bit31) is the register clock SHARED by all 22 RTC pads; IDF's rtcio_ll_function_select clears it GLOBALLY when any pin routes to digital (machine.Pin -> rtc_gpio_deinit), and the next lightsleep's EXT1 prepare re-enables it (= the self-healing). 2026-07-20 a96f1666
ULP 1.15.0: gate the hall wake to BP_RUNNING (counting unchanged) — #46 A/B
Version-diff of the aborted 40 h run against the 44 h reference found the prime suspect: ULP 1.13.0's centralized bp_hall_edge_service() calls wakeup_main_cpu() UNCONDITIONALLY from four sites — BP_RUNNING plus the dose/park drive windows, the AC busy slice and BP_PARKING entry. In 1.12.0 the BP hall wake existed ONLY inside BP_RUNNING. So since 1.13.0 every dose pokes the main CPU while it is awake (dosing polls on plain asyncio), leaving the RTC SW-CPU interrupt latched for the next machine.lightsleep() to enter against — precisely where #46 kills the board (breadcrumb 22), and precisely when the resets cluster. Rates: 0 resets / 22 sample-ends on fw 0.1.114 + ULP 1.12.0, versus 3 / 10 on fw 0.1.125 + ULP 1.13.0. Fisher exact p~0.02 — a real regression, not chance. Fix keeps what SWR-289 actually needed: crossings are still counted and timestamped in EVERY state (the multi-spot ledger depends on that), only the wake is gated. Nothing in the firmware depends on a mid-dose wake. Deliberately one variable: firmware stays 0.1.125; the ULP deploy carries the regenerated map (binary 7268 B, headroom 568 B LOW-accepted, canary armed). 1.14.0 is burned by the withdrawn breadcrumb build. 2026-07-20 20860ef1
SWR-294: transactional, import-proven firmware deploy (rollback bench-proven)
A deploy can no longer leave the board unbootable. Files stage as <dest>.new and are length+FNV verified before ANY live path is touched (an interruption there leaves the board wholly on the previous firmware); they then flip together with the outgoing copies kept as <dest>.bak; the deploy then IMPORTS the shipped surface on-board (fluispotter + synchronize.http + version read-back) and restores the .bak set on any failure. Plus a free-space pre-flight that aborts with the board untouched. Bench-proven both ways today: a normal deploy staged 12 files, flipped, smoked OK; then a DELIBERATELY broken version.py (the exact 2026-07-20 failure) was caught by the smoke test, 12 files rolled back, and the board came up on the previous firmware with no replug. verify 160 reqs, 139 PASS / 0 FAIL (288 tests); locks clean. fw 0.1.123: #46 CAUSAL FIX — console-silent nap path + latched-sink veto (crumb 22 conviction)
Campaign 4 fired at sample 2 with breadcrumb 22: the CPU died INSIDE machine.lightsleep, watchdog fed one line earlier, crumb 23 never written. The ESP-IDF sleep-entry path flushes the console TX with interrupts OFF — and a USB-CDC host that stopped draining turns that flush into a multi-second interrupts-off window (IWDT, #108 class). The nap path was writing to that console on BOTH sides of the sleep. Fix: (a) nap path is console-silent — 'Entering'/'Woke' markers retired, crumbs 22/23 carry the identical wedge signal as RTC-memory writes with zero I/O; (b) never lightsleep while serial_sink.is_latched() — the sink's own 'host stopped draining' flag, never previously consulted. Corroboration for scope: the 44 h battery run (USB DISCONNECTED) napped thousands of times with ZERO watchdog resets vs ~1-in-13 sample-ends on every USB-attached campaign — #46 is likely a bench exposure field devices never had. SWR-274 amended (DOC 1.81); SWR-245's wake-marker test re-anchored to crumb 23. verify 159 reqs, 139 PASS / 0 FAIL. Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-07-19 c77426a4
fw 0.1.119 HOTFIX: withdraw the RTC.memory breadcrumb store — it corrupted the ULP parking path (owner-diagnosed)
The 0.1.118 SWR-293 store shares RTC slow memory with the ULP; every post-boot calibration froze the ULP mid-parking with the motor left at drive power. A TRUE cold boot did not clear it — the fault was software-recreated each boot. Ticks + canary stayed intact, so the exoneration tests (idle writes, boot tick checks) missed it: the parking path was never exercised after a write. All RTC user-memory usage removed; _crumb() is a documented no-op with call sites kept for the future safe store (dedicated ULP shared variable, parking-path write-tested, next ULP build). 2026-07-18 f062f55b
ULP 1.13.0: hall crossings counted + ring-timestamped in EVERY state (SWR-289); probe-fill clamp
One centralized detector (bp_hall_edge_service, inline, shared bp_hall_prev) replaces the BP_RUNNING-only block and runs in bp_drive_window (dose+park windows), ac_busy_regulate's BP-watch poll and the BP_PARKING entry check: every physical crossing increments bp_hall_revolutions, stamps rtc_ticks (low-32) into the 9-entry bp_hall_ring (head published last), and wakes the CPU. Headroom 592 B (LOW accepted: helper inline, max depth unchanged, canary armed). FOUND+FIXED live: the firmware's stack-probe pattern-fill (fixed top-640 bytes) overlapped the top 48 B of the grown BSS and clobbered the canary sentinel - the ULP latched a false stack-overflow and halted both DACs. pump_regulator.py now clamps the fill above ULP_STACK_CANARY (top-of-BSS sentinel) so layout drift can only shrink the probe, never corrupt the ULP. variables.py regenerated via mapgen from the shipped ELF (the make copy target was bypassed by the direct builder - first deploy shipped a stale table). Bench-proven on the board: canary 0xC0FFEE5A intact, overflow 0, ticks advancing (~141 kHz), 40 s spin -> head=2/revs=2 with ring deltas 15.7 s against the 16 s/rev target. 2026-07-16 bd19b90e
fw 0.1.113: safe-off moved to boot.py (earliest software moment) + AC/BP asymmetry answered (SWR-276)
Why only the AC: both DAC channels power up at zero-scale (full power in the inverted convention), but the AC enable line (GPIO 9) rests ON through reset while the BP enable (GPIO 17) rests OFF — the pumps differ ONLY in the enable line's resting state. boot.py now performs the safe-off a few hundred ms before main.py (which repeats it); the deploy set ships boot.py. The remaining pre-boot.py window (ROM + VM init, ~0.5-1 s) is closable only in hardware: pull the AC enable down so it rests OFF like the BP's. Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-07-16 e91e6843
fw 0.1.112 CRITICAL: actuator power-on safety — AC pump span at magnet-removal power-on (SWR-276)
The DAC7574 powers up at ZERO-SCALE, which is FULL POWER in this board's inverted signal convention (power = 4095 - register); with the motor-enable line floating until firmware claims it, the AC pump ran from power application until the ULP's first tick — several seconds. The old code even skipped the DAC safe-off on power-on boots on the assumption that the POR value was safe: backwards for this convention. Fix 1: main.py _actuators_safe_off() as the FIRST statements of the entry point — motor enables (AC 9, BP 17, encoders 14/18, valve 8, tape reel 41) driven LOW and DAC CH0/CH1 commanded HALT via SoftI2C within ~10 ms of software start, before USB probing or any import. Fix 2: pump_regulator.start() DAC safe-off + I2C bus recovery unconditional. Residual pre-main.py window (ROM bootloader + VM init) is hardware scope — enable-line pull-down tracked with electronics. Registry 1.67: SWR-276 + 4 structure tests (+2 stale anchors updated to the stronger unconditional behavior). 143 reqs, 125 PASS, 0 FAIL. Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

2026-07-14 ab389f19
ULP 1.12.0: cooperative AC service through BP dose/coast/park busy loops (SWR-271)
Root cause of the 8h run's residual ~3/h false AC hall warnings: BP_DOSING / BP_DOSE_COAST / BP_PARKING hold the ULP inside blocking loops for the whole dose (~10 s), coast (5 s) and park (<=30 s) — ac_tick() never runs there, so an AC hall pass falling inside such a window was LOST outright (a lost pass reads as a 2x-SPR counter gap on the CPU monitor => false 'did not advance' warning) and the AC ran open-loop on its held DAC — the owner's masking diagnosis, confirmed in source and fixed per his 'task scheduler in the ULP Dose/Park' direction. ac_busy_hall_service(): ac_tick's exact hall block (level mirror, 1->0 count, CPU wake), now shared by ac_tick and called inside every blocking BP spin (bp_drive_window, coast, park centre-offset) — no AC revolution can be lost to a BP busy window. ac_busy_regulate(): every-16th-window (~0.8 s), 150 ms- capped, 8-interval AC Simple-P slice between BP drive windows in DOSING and PARKING; its combined poll keeps counting BP encoder edges into the caller's ledger so dose/park tick accounting stays EXACT; dose-tail guard (160 edges) keeps target-crossing out of any slice; the park slice watches the BP hall so home is never overshot; no kick/ladder/stall-recovery coupling. ac_busy_slices telemetry proves regulation ran. Gates: canary linked, no libgcc, op headroom 696 B (was 1296; LOW accepted — max call depth unchanged vs the existing regulate_pump->run_simple_p chain, .stack_canary + firmware per-tick check armed, 184 B above the 512 fail line). Control UI stuck (frozen) on PID Tuner Run; no error msg. Root cause: blocking serial I/O on the Qt UI thread — bridge.inject() (raw-REPL handshake + pump_test.bin swap) ran inline, and the serial port had NO write_timeout, so _write's flush() blocked forever when the board's USB-CDC stopped draining (wedged after the day's crashes/power-cycles) → permanent GUI freeze. Fix (host-only, no deploy): (1) serial_bridge.py opens the port with write_timeout=2.0 (connect + reconnect) so writes raise instead of hang; (2) main.py _start_pid_tuner/_stop_pid_tuner build the script on the UI thread (gate errors surface) but run inject on a daemon thread via new _inject_async(script, follow, on_fail); failures marshal back to the UI thread via a _run_on_ui = pyqtSignal(object) (queued). Result: UI never freezes; a non-responsive board → 'could not start — …'. Verified headless: _start_pid_tuner returns 0ms while inject hangs 30s; failure reported on UI thread + mode reverts. [screenshot N=32 kp3000 ki360000] Still an issue with N implementation into PID algo. Found TWO residual N-deps in 1.2.10: (a) ERR_CLAMP applied to the RAW error BEFORE normalization → usable normalized range = ERR_CLAMP·TPC/N, so at large N moderate/large slowdowns were throttled; (b) integral ran on the RAW error (∝N) → 16× larger steps at N=32 → windup/oscillation. Fix (ULP_Test 1.2.12): normalize FIRST (raw_err unclamped), clamp the NORMALIZED error (N-invariant threshold), and run P, I AND D all on e_norm. Stall still → +ERR_CLAMP kick. pid_tune_error now = raw_err (unclamped) so host _tgt=_meas-_err stays correct. At N=TPC=2 identical to before (gains transfer). 28
N=16 makes regulation go crazy — N not handled correctly in the PID. Root cause: error = measured_ticks − target_ticks scales with the window (∝ N), and P/D multiply that raw error, so loop gain ∝ N (N=16 ≈ 8× the N=2 tuning → oscillation). Fix (ULP_Test 1.2.10, test_pid_regulate only): P/D use a window-normalized error e_norm = error*TARGET_PULSE_COUNT/pulse_count (udiv64, sign-handled); INTEGRAL keeps the raw error (raw ≈ ∫err·dt, wall-clock rate already N-independent); deadband compares e_norm; pid_tune_error stays raw (host _tgt=_meas-_err). Loop now invariant to N; at N=TARGET_PULSE_COUNT(=2) identical to before so current gains hold. Operational run_pid untouched → bin byte-identical (1.2.4). Off-device verified (op byte-identical, test 4584B, headroom 3108B, only __ashldi3). PENDING DEPLOY (drives BP motor; close Control app). run_pid needs the same split when promoting.

"I am not getting any signal from the VEML spot/line sensors." ROOT CAUSE: the Control Sensors monitor script (and the Monitor MONITOR_SCRIPT) read the VEML6035 directly over I2C but (a) NEVER configured it — the VEML6035 boots SHUT DOWN (CONFIG reg 0x00 bit0=ALS_SD), so a bare read returns nothing — and (b) read the WRONG register 0x09 with a made-up scale 0.0336. The firmware's veml6035 driver (lib/MicroPython-VEML6035) powers it on via write_configuration() and reads ALS data at reg 0x04; TapeReceivers configures 25 ms IT / gain-dg normal / sensitivity HIGH → CONFIG word 0x0300, lux=count0.0512 (lux_resolution per designingveml6035.pdf). FIX (monitor/board_script.py, BOTH _veml_lux): added _veml_init(addr) writing reg 0x00=0x0300 (little-endian) to power the sensor on (called for 0x29 + 0x3A at startup + a 60 ms settle), changed the read to reg 0x04, and lux=count0.0512. Host-only injected script (no board deploy) — restart the Control app / re-open the Sensors tab to pick it up. Verified compile + generated-script content; live pyserial confirm pending (Control app holding the port). UNCOMMITTED (with 42/43).

"In control 'Pumps' The BP controls do not start the SimpleP correctly." Root cause (code-verified): the Pumps tab was built for the legacy positional kp/ki/kd PID and never arms the operational Simple-P — it wrote KP/KI/KD_SCALED + target + dac + run, with NO step_per_pct/op_p_ and no reseed. BP-SPECIFIC blocker: the ULP BP state machine has BP_DOSING and BP_PARKING states that PRE-EMPT BP_RUNNING (higher priority), so bp_pump_running=1 alone may never reach BP_RUNNING (→ Simple-P never engages). AC has no such states, which is why AC worked and BP didn't. Fix (pump_panel.py): added a _arm_preamble() mirroring the proven PID-Tuner Operational arming — clears bp_dosing/bp_parking (BP only, via pre_clear=(BP_DOSING,BP_PARKING)), stops the pump + sleep 150ms for a clean IDLE→RUNNING reseed, writes the per-pump step_per_pct (AC 7 / BP 3) + op_p_ bounds (25/64/4095/6/4) + clears motor_stop_events; then target + DAC + run=1. Power mode now writes step=0 (Simple-P holds the seeded power). Apply re-asserts step + target. Addresses getattr-guarded from variables.py (degrades on older firmware). Verified: compile OK; replicated command generation shows BP clears dosing/parking + reseed + step=3 + op_p_* + target, AC has no dosing clear + step=7. NEEDS HARDWARE TEST (host-side change; can't bench-test motor here). Control bumped 1.1.5→1.1.6. Uncommitted.

[screenshot: Pumps tab, AC clean (349 pwr, 19.98 s/rev) but BP RUNNING + oscillating to full power (~4000 spikes), 11.04 s/rev vs 16 target] v1.1.6 fixed the START (BP now reaches BP_RUNNING). New issue revealed: BP regulation oscillates. Code-confirmed cause (ULP main.c): start_power = DAC_HALT − preset_dac (init gate), and run_simple_p seeds pwr=start_power on first_update (line 1047). The Pumps tab preset DAC gave start_power=1000 — too low for BP. Sequence: BP_IDLE→RUNNING → at power 1000 BP doesn't sustain → count==0 → spin-up KICK (DAC_START → escalates to DAC_MAX_POWER, the ~4000 spikes) → spins → first run_simple_p re-seeds pwr=1000 → stalls again → re-kick → limit cycle (runs fast ~11 s/rev on the kick-heavy average). AC sustains at low power (settles 349) so AC is fine at 1000. The validated clean BP@3 used the PID Tuner with start_power=3200. FIX (v1.1.7): per-pump start power default — BP=3000 (matches board_config BLOOD_PUMP_START_POWER), AC=1000; via new _PumpCard start_power_default param. "The verification metric for the ULP SimpleP regulation is to base the SPR calculation on TIME BETWEEN HALL, not Encoder sampling time interval." hall-interval is the TRUE rev time. RATIONALE: the encoder batch (elapsed_us*5184/pulses) is a 1/324-rev sub-sample dominated by within-rev peristaltic ripple — it bounces ±10% sample-to-sample (BP read ~14.x mid-rev, only the final partial batch read 16.5; THAT single value was what triggered the earlier 17.3-vs-16 false fail). Board-side time between consecutive hall_revolutions increments = a full true revolution, which averages the ripple out. CHANGE: output/run_operational_convergence.py drive_script now tracks last-hall _lh + hall-time _ht, computes sprh=ticks_diff(now,_ht)/1000/dh on each hall increment, emits sprh in the op frame (25 ms poll for edge resolution); main() summary reports sprh_mean (mean of hall intervals, first/spin-up discarded). verification/tests_mech/test_bench.py grades sprh_mean (not the encoder-batch tail-mean) against ±5%, requiring ≥3 hall intervals + stop==0 + hall>0. SWR-122/123 text now SPECIFIES the hall-interval metric; registry DOC_VERSION 1.1→1.2; docs/fluispotter_sw_requirements.html+.docx regenerated. HARDWARE (device on /dev/cu.usbmodem101, apps closed, pyserial RawRepl): AC @20: sprh_mean=19.808 (−0.96%), 3 hall-intervals 19.78/19.83/19.83, pwr 421, skip 6, stop 0. BP @16: sprh_mean=16.000 (0.00%), 4 hall-intervals 16.06/15.97/16.04/15.94, pwr ~2700, skip→6, stop 0 — vs encoder-batch bouncing 14.31–16.57 over the same run (the metric's whole point). Formal MECH suite: SWR-122/123/124/125 ALL PASS (4/4). Pumps reset idle, stop=0. "And Brown-Out-Detect?" / "Is there any reason NOT to enable BOD?" / "You say WDT but I dont think we enabled the WDT — it collided with the lightsleep and tripped just because we were sleeping." / "I dont think we enabled BOD." / "Note this: WIFI and EEPROM writes are busy tasks that will bind the CPU. Are we sure a log write and a WIFI comms cannot collide? We introduced an interrupt to wake the CPU from lightsleep — what happens if that interrupt hits while we are both writing and WIFI updating?" register read RTC_CNTL_BROWN_OUT_REG 0x600080D4 = 0x02719000: BOD ENA/RST_ENA = 0 → confirmed DISABLED. Enabling BOD is orthogonal safety (no downside for a mains/USB-powered medical device); it also converts any real sag to a clean brownout reset instead of a silent glitch. Sten's WIFI+EEPROM-bind-the-CPU is the ground truth (memory wifi_flash_cpu_binding); the wake-interrupt-during-flash-write hazard is the collision class the IWDT margin protects against. Mechanism guidance accepted.

153. 2026-07-17 — "Hub only partially updates automatically... stale ONLINE status visible."

·
> ROOT CAUSE: the ONLINE/OFFLINE badge renders from access_points.connected, which is written
·
by /usr/bin/synchronized-hostapd-handler — a SHELL SCRIPT hostapd_cli spawns on (dis)associate,
·
writing sqlite DIRECTLY. The service process never observes that mutation, so it never SSE-
·
broadcasts: DB correct, open pages stale (ONLINE stuck until manual refresh; ONLINE-side often
·
looked live only because the device's first sync POST triggered its own broadcast). FIX hub
·
1.7.3 (fef85a6, LIVE): _connectivity_watch background task in http/server.py polls the hostapd
·
station list every 8 s, set-diffs MACs, looks up joined/left devices (devices.mac_address,
·
6-byte bytes) and sse.broadcast()s each — OFFLINE now propagates to open pages within ~8 s,
·
no page code changed. Verified live: board napping mid-session, station list without its MAC,
·
page badge OFFLINE on v1.7.3 footer; the running session's next wake/nap flip is the natural

171. 2026-07-18 — "verify that the deque is updated EVERY TIME the BP passes the Hall"

·
> VERIFIED: NO — bp_hall_revolutions++ exists at exactly ONE site (main.c:1997, case BP_RUNNING
·
only). BP_DOSING/BP_PARKING read the hall solely as a stop condition (bp_drive_window
·
watch_hall/hall_found; parking arrival) — crossings there are DETECTED but never counted, never
·
woken. Each dose cycle crosses the hall once (during park) -> tube advances one segment
·
invisibly to the ledger. CPU deque (7 entries) counts deltas losslessly but batches timestamps
·
(N revs seen in one poll share one stamp — minor skew). Doses 2..N start BLIND (no deque
·
consult). Impact bounded honestly: dose 1 of well-separated samples always correct (gate window
·
contains only RUNNING revs — why all audits were clean); intra-set doses hit non-adjacent
·
segments + the full-rev delay; 2-min tables marginal (park crossing can fall in the next gate
·
window). Plan v2: ULP counts crossings in EVERY state (SWR-289) + intra-set gate on ring
·
advance (SWR-288).

172. 2026-07-18 — "Can the Deque be entirely populated by the ULP?" + multi-spot report bug (6 timepoints, 10 spots)

·
head-last ordering, ~20 shared words) — kills BOTH deque defects (uncounted dose/park revs +
·
batched timestamps); py reduces to ring read + ticks->wall conversion; decision stays CPU-side.
·
MULTI-SPOT BUG diagnosed as systemic one-spot-per-timepoint assumptions: samples_taken() =
·
newest sample INDEX+1 (timepoint, not spots), denominators = len(time_table) not sum(samples),
